Let me think about the task. This is the classic TAPL-style STLC strong normalization proof using a logical relation (hereditarily terminating terms) with locally nameless representation.

Key challenges:
1. The logical relation needs to handle open terms with free variables (fvar atoms). Since typing is with respect to a context, we need a context interpretation: for each x with Gamma x = Some T, the term `tm_fvar x` is in the relation at type T.
2. Terms can contain free variables (fvars). The SN relation needs to accommodate them: e.g., `tm_fvar x` is a value and can't step, so it's SN. Applications of fvars can step only when arguments step, etc.

Actually the standard approach in TAPL's "Strong Normalization" chapter (Chapter 12) uses a unary relation on closed terms... but note "closed" here means locally closed, not free-variable-closed. The T_Abs rule opens with a fresh fvar. So terms in the relation may contain fvars. We can define the relation on all terms:

- R_Bool = { t | SN t ∧ (value t or ...) } — standard TAPL defines R_T for closed terms as:
  - R_Bool = { t closed | t is a value or t is SN... } Actually TAPL's definition: 
    - R_Bool = {t | t is closed and t is a normal form or t reduces to a normal form... } Hmm, let me recall.

TAPL Chapter 12 (Strong Normalization): The logical relation is defined on closed terms:
- R_Bool = { t | t ∈ SN ∧ (t is a value or t →* true or t →* false) }? Actually TAPL defines:
  - R_Bool = { t | t is closed ∧ (t is a value or t →* true or t →* false)? }

Hmm, let me remember. TAPL 12.2: Define RA for closed terms:
- R_Bool = { t | t ∈ SN and (t is a value or t →* true or t →* false) }? Actually I recall simply:
  - R_Bool = { t | t is closed and t ∈ SN and t is either a value or reduces to a value }?

But terms in the relation may be open with fvars. Define the logical relation on all terms (with possible fvars/bvars):

Fixpoint R (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => SN t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t => SN t /\ forall s, R T1 s -> R T2 (tm_app t s)
  end.

Lemma sn_app_fvar : forall ts, SN ts -> forall t x, ts = tm_app t (tm_fvar x) -> SN t.
Proof.
  intros ts H. induction H as [ts Hstep IH]. intros t x Heq. subst ts.
  constructor. intros t' Ht'.
  assert (Hstep' : (tm_app t (tm_fvar x)) --> tm_app t' (tm_fvar x)) by (apply ST_App1; auto using lc_fvar).
  apply (IH _ Hstep'). reflexivity.
Qed.

Now, is the definition R_Bool := SN and arrow as above actually sufficient for the fundamental theorem? Let's think about the T_Abs case in detail.

Goal: prove "fundamental theorem": forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> forall sigma, (forall x T', Gamma x = Some T' -> R T' (sigma x)) -> R T (subst sigma t)? Hmm, we need substitution. Maybe simpler: define the "context interpretation" via fvars: R_Gamma(t) := forall sigma... Hmm.

Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> forall sigma, (forall x, ...) -> ...

But we need a substitution operation. There isn't one in the file (only open). We can define our own substitution:

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)  (* note: binder is locally nameless with named fvars, so substitution under abs is fine *)
  ...
  end.

Hmm, but there's a subtlety: the T_Abs rule opens t1 with a fresh fvar other than L. So when we prove the fundamental theorem by induction on the typing derivation, in the T_Abs case we have the hypothesis `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2`. We need to show R_{T1→T2}(abs T1 t1) under the context interpretation for Gamma. In particular for s ∈ R_{T1}: R_{T2}((abs T1 t1) s) and SN.

Hmm, this requires showing `open t1 s` ∈ R_{T2} for values s ∈ R_{T1}. And `open t1 (fvar x)` ∈ R_{T2} for fresh x under extended interpretation. How do we bridge from the fvar version to the general s version? That's the "substitution lemma": if for fresh x, open t1 (fvar x) is in the relation under interpretation where x ↦ (something in R_{T1}), then open t1 s ∈ R_{T2}.

Approach: generalize the fundamental theorem to contexts represented as functions atom -> tm (substitutions), and prove a substitution lemma relating open and subst.

Let me think about a well-known technique that works smoothly: define the logical relation on *closed* (locally closed) terms and prove "closure under beta" via a substitution lemma over the opening operation. Let me just search my memory differently. There's a standard exercise/known Coq proof for STLC SN with logical relations. Common formulation:

Inductive SN : tm -> Prop := ...
Definition LR (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Bool => fun t => SN t
  | T1 → T2 => fun t => SN t /\ (forall s, LR T1 s -> LR T2 (app t s))
  end.

Then prove:
1. LR T t -> SN t.
2. LR T t -> forall t', step t t' -> LR T t'  (closure under reduction) — actually needed.
3. "Expansion"/neutral: if t is neutral (i.e., not a value and all reducts are in LR) then t ∈ LR.

Hmm, but for CBV the standard "neutral" is: terms that are not values but whose reducts... Consider `fvar x`: not a value, no reducts, so vacuously neutral. Consider `app t1 t2` where t1 is neutral... For LR at arrow type we need SN plus app-preservation.

Let me try a slightly different, more robust formulation tailored to this CBV step relation. Key observations about this language's step relation:

- Values: abs (locally closed), true, false.
- Step: beta (when arg is a value), App1 (when arg is locally closed), App2 (when function is a value).

A term is SN iff ... Let's define a notion that makes the proof smooth:

Define LR T t := 
  match T with
  | Bool => SN t
  | Arrow T1 T2 => SN t /\ forall s, LR T1 s -> LR T2 (app t s)
  end.

We need the fundamental theorem with contexts. Let's think about what's needed:

For T_Abs case: We have body typing under extended context for fresh fvars. We need: forall s ∈ LR T1, LR T2 (app (abs T1 body) s).

Key sub-lemma A: If body' is locally closed and (for all values v ∈ LR T1, LR T2 (open body' v)) and SN body'... then app (abs body) s ∈ LR T2 for s ∈ LR T1.

To prove LR T2 (app abs s) with s ∈ LR T1, we need:
(i) SN (app abs s)
(ii) for T2 = arrow: forall u ∈ LR T2_1, LR T2_2 (app abs s u), which follows from (i)-style reasoning plus the definitional unfolding: app abs s u = app (app abs s) u and if we know LR_{T2_1→T2_2}(app abs s) then we get it. So the recursive structure forces us to prove LR at all types simultaneously.

  | inert_app : forall t1 t2, inert t1 -> inert (tm_app t1 t2).

Lemmas:
- inert_not_value : inert t -> value t -> False. (induction; all cases absurd by inversion on value)
- inert_no_step : inert t -> no_step t. Induction on inert.
  - fvar: step (fvar x) t' — inversion on step: all conclusions are apps; fvar ≠ app. 
  - bvar same.
  - app t1 t2 with inert t1: inversion on step: AppAbs requires t1 = abs T t0 — but inert t1 and inert_not_value gives value (abs T t0)?? Wait in ST_AppAbs, the term is `tm_app (tm_abs T t) v`, so t1 = tm_abs T t. And inert (tm_abs T t) would be needed — but inert is only built from fvar/bvar/app, so inert (abs ...) is impossible by inversion. Actually inversion on inert t1 = abs directly gives contradiction since inert has no abs constructor. 
  - App1: t1 --> t1' contradicts IH.
  - App2: value t1 contradicts inert_not_value.
  
  Good.
- inert_app closure: inert t -> inert (app t s)? From inert_app constructor directly! inert_app : inert t1 -> inert (app t1 t2). Yes! So inert is closed under app by construction. 

- inert_SN : inert t -> SN t. Since inert_no_step gives SN via constructor with no reducts.
- inert_LR : forall T t, inert t -> LR T t. By induction on T:
  - Bool: SN ✓.
  - Arrow A B: SN ✓; forall s, LR A s -> LR B (app t s). Apply IH B with inert (app t s) ✓ (inert_app).
  Wait, IH B: forall t, inert t -> LR B t. So apply to app t s. ✓ 

So inert terms are in LR at all types. 

Now back to the Abs case. We have LR T2 (open t1 (fvar x)) for all fresh x. Want: forall s ∈ LR T1, LR T2 (app (abs T1 t1) s).

Case 1: locally_closed (abs T1 t1) is false. Then app (abs T1 t1) s has no steps; but is it inert? No — inert is syntactic. But we can prove directly: LR T2 (app (abs T1 t1) s) via a bespoke lemma:

Lemma LR_nonlc_abs : forall T A body s, ~ locally_closed (tm_abs A body) -> LR T (tm_app (tm_abs A body) s).
Proof: induction on T.
  - Bool: SN (app abs s): no steps (prove no_step: inversion on step; AppAbs would need lc; App1 needs abs step — abs never steps; App2 needs value abs = lc). 
  - Arrow A' B: SN ✓; forall u ∈ LR A', LR B (app (app abs s) u). Hmm, need a generalized statement for iterated applications. The term app (app abs s) u has no steps (prove directly: App1 needs app abs s step (no); App2 needs value (app abs s) (no); AppAbs needs head abs (no)). So maybe generalize: for any list of args applied to a non-lc abs, the term is normal. Then prove by induction on T that such terms are in LR. Hmm, or simpler: prove "forall T t, no_step t -> ... LR T t" — but no_step alone isn't enough for LR at arrow: we need forall u ∈ LR A', LR B (app t u); and app t u may step if t is a value (e.g., t = abs lc!). Right, no_step isn't enough.

Hmm, but there's a cleaner way to handle Case 1: Note LR T2 (open t1 (fvar x)) — hmm, that doesn't give us lc info.

Alternative approach to Case 1: Prove LR T (app (abs A body) s) by induction on T with a generalized "head" notion:

Define a predicate `stuck_head` on terms meaning "the term is an application headed by a non-value, non-stepping term, with arbitrary arguments, or an fvar/bvar, with arbitrary arguments":

Inductive head_stuck : tm -> Prop :=
  | hs_fvar : forall x, head_stuck (tm_fvar x)
  | hs_bvar : forall i, head_stuck (tm_bvar i)
  | hs_app : forall t1 t2, head_stuck t1 -> head_stuck (tm_app t1 t2)
  | hs_nonlc_abs : forall A body, ~ locally_closed (tm_abs A body) -> head_stuck (tm_abs A body)
  | hs_nonlc_abs_app : forall A body s, ~ lc (abs A body) -> head_stuck (tm_app (tm_abs A body) s)? 

Hmm, but to prove hs_app (app (abs_nonlc) s) we need closure under app: head_stuck t -> head_stuck (app t s) requires that t is not a *lc abs*. Let's define:

Inductive hs : tm -> Prop :=
  | hs_fvar : forall x, hs (tm_fvar x)
  | hs_bvar : forall i, hs (tm_bvar i)
  | hs_nlc_abs : forall A body, ~ locally_closed (tm_abs A body) -> hs (tm_abs A body)
  | hs_app : forall t1 t2, hs t1 -> hs (tm_app t1 t2).

Closure under app: hs t -> hs (app t s) — by hs_app ✓ automatically.

Now prove: hs t -> not_value t. Check each case:
- fvar, bvar: not value ✓
- nlc_abs: not value ✓ (v_abs requires lc)
- app: not value ✓.

Prove: hs t -> no_step t. Induction:
- fvar/bvar: no step ✓.
- nlc_abs: no step (abs never steps) ✓.
- app t1 t2 with hs t1: inversion on step:
  - ST_AppAbs: t1 = tm_abs T t0, and lc (abs T t0) — but hs (abs T t0) means either hs_nlc_abs (contradicts lc, actually ~lc vs lc contradiction) or hs_app/hs_fvar/hs_bvar — but abs is not fvar/bvar/app, so only hs_nlc_abs applies; contradiction ✓.
  - ST_App1: t1 --> t1' contradicts IH (no_step t1) ✓.
  - ST_App2: value t1 contradicts not_value t1 ✓.

Then: hs t -> LR T t, by induction on T:
- Bool: SN t ✓ (no_step → SN).
- Arrow A B: SN ✓; forall s, LR A s -> LR B (app t s): apply IH B to (app t s) which is hs by hs_app ✓.

So this covers both fvar-headed and non-lc-abs-headed terms. 

Now Case 2: locally_closed (abs T1 t1) holds. Then `app (abs T1 t1) s` steps via App2 (s → s') and AppAbs (when s is a value) to `open t1 s`.

Lemma LR_app_abs : forall s, SN s -> forall A body B,

  (forall v, value v -> LR A v -> LR B (open body v)) ->
  LR B (tm_app (tm_abs A body) s).

The standard Tait "expansion lemma": if t is not a value... hmm. Let me look at this from the classic Girard proof: Define the relation, prove:
(i) t ∈ R_T ⟹ SN t
(ii) t ∈ R_T ⟹ every reduct of t is in R_T (CR1/CR2 style: if t ∈ R and t →* t' then t' ∈ R)
(iii) neutral terms: if t is neutral and all its one-step reducts are in R_T then t ∈ R_T.

With CBV, app (fvar x) u is neutral/stuck ✓, app (abs_nonlc) u is stuck ✓, app (app h s) u with h stuck ✓. So the hs predicate is exactly "neutral" here (stuck terms)! But in our specific use case, we DO know LR B (open body v) for all values v ∈ LR A. So let's set up the specific lemma:

Claim(β') For all s with SN s and LR A s, for all A body B,
   lc (abs A body) ->
   (forall v, value v -> LR A v -> LR B (open body v)) ->
   LR B (app (abs A body) s).

Proof by induction on SN s. To prove LR B (app abs s), we need at type B:
- if B = Bool: SN (app abs s). Proof as before works: reducts are open body s (when value s) → LR Bool = SN ✓ (hypothesis gives LR B (open body s) since LR A s ✓), or app abs s' → IH gives LR B → SN ✓. So SN ✓. So LR Bool ✓.
- if B = Arrow B1 B2: need SN (same as above, SN is type-independent — works with the same argument: reducts are app abs s' (IH gives LR B (app abs s') for the full B, hence SN) and open body s (hypothesis gives LR B, hence SN)) ✓; plus ∀u ∈ LR B1, LR B2 (app (app abs s) u).

For the last: consider the term T_s := app (abs A body) s. We know LR B T_s. Then by the arrow clause of LR B (B = B1→B2), we immediately get ∀u ∈ LR B1, LR B2 (T_s u) ✓✓. 

Oh wait! That's circular but in a good way: LR B (app abs s) is what we're proving, and its arrow clause gives the ∀u part. So the structure of the proof is: to prove LR B (app abs s) at arrow B, we need SN and ∀u ∈ LR B1, LR B2 (app abs s u). The latter can't come from the arrow clause of the thing we're proving. Hmm, right, circular.

So we need to prove ∀u ∈ LR B1, LR B2 (app abs s u) independently. Hmm.

OK so let's think about `app (app (abs A body) s) u` where u ∈ LR B1. This term's reducts:

  - AppAbs: no.
So reducts come from App1 only. If s is a value v: the head app abs v steps to open body v (when? AppAbs requires value v ✓ and lc abs ✓). So app (app abs v) u → app (open body v) u. We know LR B (open body v), so by the arrow clause, LR B2 (app (open body v) u) ✓.
If s → s': reducts are app (app abs s') u; by IH on SN s, LR B (app abs s')... hmm, IH gives LR B (app abs s') for the full B (since IH is for the same B), which by arrow clause gives LR B2 (app (app abs s') u) ✓.

Main claim: LR B (app abs s) — prove by induction on type B:
  - B = Bool: SN ✓ as above.
  - B = B1 → B2: need SN ✓ and ∀u ∈ LR B1, LR B2 (app (app abs s) u).
    To prove the latter for u ∈ LR B1: we want LR B2 (app (app abs s) u). By induction on type B2? This is getting complicated. Let's step back and think about the standard proof more holistically.

The standard Tait proof works with a *unary* relation on terms and proves that the relation is closed under beta expansion via the "CR" properties. The cleanest formulation I know for CBV:

Define LR_T ⊆ terms (all terms, possibly open), with:
- LR_Bool = {t | SN t}
- LR_{A→B} = {t | SN t ∧ ∀s ∈ LR_A, t s ∈ LR_B}

Prove:
(1) LR_T t → SN t.
(2) LR_T is closed under reduction: LR_T t → t → t' → LR_T t'. [Needs lc? maybe add lc to LR]
(3) If t is a term such that all its reducts are in LR_T and t is SN, then t ∈ LR_T? — we saw this fails at arrow without beta knowledge.

Hmm, alternatively, use a different tactic: prove the fundamental theorem with the relation defined via "SN + valuation" but handle the abstraction case by defining the relation on values only and then extending to arbitrary terms as "SN + reduction to a value" — standard for CBV!

CBV standard approach ("logical relation in the style of Plotkin): 
- Define a relation on *values*: V_T.
- For general terms: t ∈ LR_T iff SN t and every "value reduct" (t →* v) is in V_T.
- Then app: if t1 ∈ LR_{A→B} and t2 ∈ LR_A, show t1 t2 ∈ LR_B: reducts of t1 t2 are either t1' t2, t1 t2', or open body v with v a value.

Hmm, let's think about this. Define:

V_Bool(v) := True (values true/false... but what about abs? Bool values are true/false).
V_{A→B}(v) := ∀ s ∈ LR_A, LR_B (v s)? Or ∀ v' ∈ V_A, LR_B (v v')?

and LR_T(t) := SN t ∧ ∀ v, t →* v → V_T(v).

Then:
- LR_T → SN ✓ (part of def).
- Values: v ∈ V_T → LR_T v? Need SN v ✓ (values are SN since they don't step) and v →* v' → V_T v' — need V_T closed under reduction... values don't reduce, so trivial. But wait, v →* v' with v a value means v' = v, so need V_T v → V_T v ✓. So yes, LR_T v iff SN v ✓ (values SN).

- App lemma: LR_{A→B}(t1), LR_A(t2) → LR_B(t1 t2). Need SN(t1 t2) and all value-reducts in V_B.
  Value reducts of t1 t2: by induction on the multistep derivation... The standard argument: every reduction path from t1 t2 either stays at the "spine" (t1, t2 reduce) or takes the beta step:
    - t1 t2 → t1' t2 →* v: use IH with LR_{A→B} t1' (need closure of LR under step!) 
    - t1 t2 → t1 t2' →* v: use IH with LR_A t2'.
    - t1 = abs A body, t2 value v, → open body v →* v_final: need LR_B (open body v), which comes from V_{A→B}(abs A body) applied to... V requires LR_A of the argument ✓ (t2 ∈ LR_A) giving LR_B (abs body t2) — but that's the very term! Hmm, V_{A→B}(abs) := ∀s ∈ LR_A, LR_B (abs s). Then LR_B (abs s) includes all value-reducts of `abs s`. And `abs s` → open body s when s is a value. So from LR_B(abs s) we get V_B of its value reducts, including `open body s`... but LR_B(abs s) is what we're proving (for term t1 t2 = abs s). Circular again.

Ugh. The fundamental circularity: the app lemma at arrow type requires the very statement.

Let's think again about the standard proof structure. In Girard-Tait, the lemma is proven by induction on the *derivation* or by a well-founded measure. The key is: to prove LR_{A→B}(t1 t2) at arrow type, we need ∀u, LR_{B1}(t1 t2 u), and that's proven by induction on the SN derivations of t1, t2, u (the "candidates of reducibility" proof: if t1 ∈ CR_{A→B} and t2 ∈ CR_A then t1 t2 ∈ CR_B — this is exactly closure under application which is part of the definition of CR for arrow types... 

OH WAIT. Hold on. In the standard Girard definition, CR_{A→B} = {t | ∀s ∈ CR_A, t s ∈ CR_B} is the DEFINITION. The application lemma is then trivially: t1 ∈ CR_{A→B} and t2 ∈ CR_A implies t1 t2 ∈ CR_B — by definition! So in the fundamental theorem's App case, it's immediate.

The hard part is proving that abstractions are in CR_{A→B}: need ∀s ∈ CR_A, (abs body) s ∈ CR_B. And that's proven by induction on the structure of B, using:
- If B = Bool (or base): SN of (abs body) s.
- If B = B1→B2: need ∀u ∈ CR_{B1}, (abs body) s u ∈ CR_{B2}. Here we use the IH at B2? No...

Hmm, in Girard's proof (for System F / simply typed lambda calculus in call-by-name), the abstraction lemma goes:
λx.M ∈ CR_{A→B} iff ∀N ∈ CR_A, M[x:=N] ∈ CR_B. This is the *definition* used in many presentations: CR_{A→B} := { M | ∀N ∈ CR_A, M[x:=N] ∈ CR_B } for terms in "long form"? Different presentations define CR on terms via substitutions.

Alternative clean presentation (the "reducibility candidates" à la Girard with the CR1-CR3 conditions):
- CR_A is a set of terms closed under reduction and with the "neutral expansion" property.
- CR_{A→B} := { M | ∀N ∈ CR_A, M N ∈ CR_B } — needs CR_B to be a candidate.
- Then to show λx.M ∈ CR_{A→B}: take N ∈ CR_A, show (λx.M) N ∈ CR_B. By the neutral/expansion property of CR_B (CR3), it suffices to show all one-step reducts of (λx.M)N are in CR_B: reducts are (λx.M')N (M → M'), (λx.M)N' (N → N'), and M[x:=N]. The first two by CR closure properties (M ∈ CR_{A→B}... hmm, need M ∈ CR_{A→B}? No: we need (λx.M')N ∈ CR_B, and λx.M' ∈ CR_{A→B} if M' ∈ CR... hmm.

I think the cleanest known formalization for STLC SN in Coq that I can reconstruct: use a *hereditarily strongly normalizing* predicate with explicit application closure, and prove the fundamental theorem by induction on typing derivations with a context substitution interpretation, where the abstraction case uses a separate lemma proven by induction on the type.

Let's think about the structure of `app (abs A body) s u1 u2 ... un` (left-associated). Its only possible reducts:
  - via the head: if s is a value v, then `(abs A body) v` → `open body v`, so `(((abs A body) v) u1...)` → `((open body v) u1...)`. Also if s → s', the head app steps (App1 on the head: app (abs) s → app (abs) s'), giving `(app abs s') u1...`.
  - via the arguments u_i stepping (App1: the function part steps — that's the head recursion; App2: the LAST argument steps if the function part is a value — but the function part is an app, never a value, so no App2 for outer apps).

So for the term T := app (app (abs A body) s) u: reducts are app (app abs s') u (head steps) or app (open body v) u (beta). Both are "apply term to u".

So let's formalize a general lemma:

Lemma LR_of_reducts: forall B t,
  locally_closed t ->
  SN t ->
  (∀ t', t --> t' → LR B t') →
  LR B t.

That's an "opening lemma": the relation at type B is closed under substitution of fvar by related terms, or more precisely, if the body (with a fresh fvar) is related for all fresh fvars... So we need the substitution lemma: LR A s → (for fresh x, LR B (open body (fvar x))) → LR B (open body s).

This is a fundamental lemma about the logical relation: "the relation is closed under substitution of related terms for free variables". Standard proof: by induction on the type B, using the structure of LR and the fact that the body with fvar x is related for ALL fresh x — actually we need it for one fresh x, plus the fact that the body doesn't contain x... Lemma LR_subst: forall T t x s, LR T (tm_fvar x) -> no wait.

We need: LR T t and LR A s → LR T (subst_fvar x s t), for x a variable not bound... In our locally nameless setting with fvars, substitution of fvar x by s in t. Hmm, so this won't be a simple induction on types. We need a stronger induction hypothesis that handles the substitution of related terms. This is exactly the classic "substitution lemma" proven by induction on types with the *closure properties* of the relation. In Girard's proof, CR2 (closure under reduction) and CR3 (neutral terms) are proven for each type, and then the substitution lemma for the relation follows by induction on the type: 

Lemma (Substitution): if σ maps variables to terms with σ(x) ∈ CR_{Γ(x)}, and Γ ⊢ M : T, then M[σ] ∈ CR_T.

For our relation, with the application-based arrow clause, how does the substitution lemma go? Let's think about what's needed: we have LR B (open body (fvar x)) and LR A s, want LR B (open body s). Let's think about the structure of the problem. The relation LR T t requires lc t ∧ SN t ∧ ∀u ∈ LR A, LR B (t u). Substituting related terms for free variables and preserving this... 

Standard approach in the literature for CBV STLC SN via logical relations with "open terms": Define
  t ∈ R_T[Γ]  iff  for all closing substitutions σ (with σ(x) ∈ R_{Γ(x)} for fvars, and the substitution is "closing"), subst σ t ∈ R_T (on closed terms).

But the "closing" requirement (closed under substitution) makes the abstraction case work by definition: For t = abs A body, R_{A→B}[Γ](abs A body) iff for all σ (related), (abs A body)[σ] ∈ R_{A→B} (closed relation), iff for all σ and all s ∈ R_A, ((abs A body)[σ]) s ∈ R_B. And ((abs A body)[σ]) s → (body[σ]) s ... but locally nameless: body under the binder; substituting σ for free vars is fine (no capture since binders are bvar) — so (abs A body)[σ] s → substitute bvar 0 by s in body[σ] = (open body s)[σ]? Hmm, since substitution of fvars and opening at bvars commute (body[σ] with bvar 0 opened by s = (open body s)[σ] as long as σ doesn't introduce bvar 0-free issues... since σ terms may contain bvars? If σ maps to locally closed terms, fine).

Then this reduces to showing R_B[(open body s)[σ]] which, if we're proving "for all σ related", requires R_B[Γ](open body s) — but that's for the term `open body s` where s is a *particular closed related term*, not a variable. Hmm, the inductive hypothesis (from typing) is about `open body (fvar x)` in context x:T1,Γ.

I think we should structure the fundamental theorem as: for all Γ, t, T, has_type Γ t T → for all σ (substitution on fvars), (∀ x T', Γ x = Some T' → LR T' (σ x)) → LR T (subst σ t).

Let me step back and consider a MUCH simpler strategy specific to this problem: since the language is tiny and there are no base-type constants except true/false (which are inert), maybe we can prove SN more directly via a "strong normalization by reducibility of terms with fvars" and handle the abstraction case using the fvar-hypothesis with a *special trick*:

Lemma: LR T (open body (fvar x)) for fresh x ⟹ LR T (open body s) for s ∈ LR T1?

via an induction on T *plus* a clever choice: use the relation's own structure. Let's attempt the induction on type T:

So the proof must use typing essentially. The way to keep typing info is to prove the fundamental theorem as the main induction and handle the abstraction case *within* it.

OK let me look at how people actually formalize STLC SN with locally nameless in Coq. Since we can't search the web, let me reason it out.

Actually, here's a cleaner idea that I think will work well: **define the logical relation with an explicit environment/substitution interpretation attached, a.k.a. "semantic typing"**:

Define, for a context Γ:
  env_ok Γ σ := forall x T, Γ x = Some T → LR T (σ x)
and
  sem_typing Γ t T := forall σ, env_ok Γ σ → LR T (subst σ t).

Then prove the fundamental theorem by induction on the typing derivation. The T_Abs case:
  Need: forall σ, env_ok Γ σ → LR_{T1→T2}(abs T1 (subst σ body)).
  We have IH: forall x fresh, forall σ', env_ok (x|->T1;Γ) σ' → LR T2 (subst σ' (open body (fvar x))).

  Take σ with env_ok Γ σ. Need LR_{T1→T2}(abs T1 (subst σ body)):
  - lc: need locally_closed (abs T1 (subst σ body)). Hmm, this requires σ's terms to be lc (env_ok gives LR → lc ✓) and the body... we need lc_at 1 body? From the IH we can get lc of subst σ' (open body (fvar x)) for a particular σ' — does that give lc(abs T1 body)? Similar to before: lc (open body (fvar x)) with x fresh... but now subst σ' also substitutes x! Choose σ' = [x ↦ fvar x] + σ. Then subst σ' (open body (fvar x)) contains fvar x possibly. lc of that... Then lc_at 1 body should follow. This seems doable but delicate.
  
  - SN (abs T1 (subst σ body)) ✓.
  - ∀ s ∈ LR T1, LR T2 (app (abs T1 (subst σ body)) s).

Alternative formulation via a *generalized environment*: Define "closing substitution" semantics where the interpretation of a context Γ is a substitution ρ mapping each variable to a term, with the property that ρ(x) ∈ LR_{Γ(x)}. Then define:

  LR_ctx Γ t T := forall ρ, (forall x T', Γ x = Some T' -> LR T' (ρ x)) -> LR T (subst ρ t)

Hmm, but the fundamental theorem for T_Abs: we need the relation for `abs T1 body` under ρ, and the body hypothesis is about `open body (fvar x)` under ρ' where ρ'(x) = fvar x and ρ' agrees with ρ elsewhere. So this works IF we can handle substitution/commutation correctly. The key insight: define the semantics with arbitrary environments (locally closed terms), not just fvar environments. Then the abstraction case works.

  - lc ✓

The IH for the body typing is: forall ρ', env_ok (x|->T1;Γ) ρ' → LR T2 (subst ρ' (open body (fvar x))). Note ρ' can be ANY environment. Yes if the substitution commutes: subst ρ' (open body (fvar x)) = open (subst ρ body) s, provided (i) ρ' (x) = s and ρ' agrees with ρ elsewhere, and (ii) the substitution doesn't interact with bvars. Since subst replaces fvars only and open replaces bvars only, they commute. But there's a subtlety: `open body (fvar x)` replaces bvar 0 with fvar x at depth 0; then subst ρ' replaces fvar x with s wherever it occurs — including occurrences of fvar x that were ALREADY in body (if x ∈ FV(body))! So: general environment approach. Let's define:

Definition subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm := ...

Actually, maybe better: define substitution as a *simultaneous* substitution:

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Note: no capture issues with bvars since binders are bvar-based; but if sigma maps to terms with free bvars, they might get "captured" by enclosing binders in the locally nameless sense. We'll require sigma terms to be locally closed when needed.

Now, the semantic typing definition:

Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> LR T (sigma x).

Definition sem_has_type (Gamma : context) (t : tm) (T : ty) : Prop :=
  forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR T (subst sigma t).

Fundamental theorem: has_type Gamma t T -> sem_has_type Gamma t T.

Then the target: empty |-- t \in T → LR T t (with sigma = identity). Need: subst identity t = t and env_ok empty identity (vacuously). So normalization follows from LR T t → SN t.

By definition: locally_closed (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma body)) ∧ SN (...) ∧ ∀ s, LR T1 s -> LR T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma body)) s).

Let me define the "update" for substitutions:

Definition subst_update (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (v : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then v else sigma y.

Then subst_update sigma x v agrees with sigma away from x, and maps x to v.

Lemma subst_update_eq: forall sigma x v, subst_update sigma x v x = v.
Lemma subst_update_neq: forall sigma x v y, x <> y -> subst_update sigma x v y = sigma y.

Lemma subst_open_fvar: forall t x sigma v,
  x ∉ FV t ->

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) (x : atom) : Prop :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => False
  | tm_fvar y => x = y
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 x \/ fv t2 x
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1 x
  | tm_true | tm_false => False
  end.

Fixpoint max_list (l : list nat) : nat :=
  match l with [] => 0 | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs) end.

Lemma max_list_ub: forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_list l.

Now, T_Abs case details. Let me write out the proof of the fundamental theorem:

Theorem fundamental: forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> sem_has_type Gamma t T.

Actually, let's define sem_has_type directly as the goal and prove by induction on the typing derivation:

forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR T (subst sigma t).

Constructors:
- T_Var: Gamma x = Some T, H : Gamma x = Some T. Goal: LR T (subst sigma (fvar x)) = LR T (sigma x) ✓ from env_ok Gamma sigma x T H.
- T_True: LR Bool (subst sigma true) = LR Bool true. Need: lc true ✓, SN true ✓, Bool so no more.
- T_False: same.
- T_App: IH1: forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR (T1→T2) (subst sigma t1); IH2: ... LR T1 (subst sigma t2).
  Goal: LR T2 (subst sigma (app t1 t2)) = LR T2 (app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)).
  From IH1: LR (T1→T2) (subst sigma t1) → its arrow clause with s := subst sigma t2 (LR T1 ✓ from IH2) gives LR T2 (app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)) ✓.
- T_Abs: as discussed.

Let's detail the T_Abs case:

Context:
  L : list atom
  Gamma : context
  T1 T2 : ty
  t1 : tm
  H : forall x : atom, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2

  sigma : atom -> tm
  Henv : env_ok Gamma sigma
  Goal: LR (T1 → T2) (subst sigma (tm_abs T1 t1)) = LR (T1→T2) (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1))

Steps:
1. Choose fresh x: x ∉ L and x ∉ fv_list t1. Let's get x with Hx: ~ In x L and Hxfv: ~ In x (fv_list t1) using a combined list L ++ fv_list t1.
2. Define sigma0 := subst_update sigma x (fvar x).
3. Henv0: env_ok (update Gamma x T1) sigma0.
   For y T', Hupd : update Gamma x T1 y = Some T'. Unfold update. If y = x then Some T1 = Some T' → T' = T1, and sigma0 y = fvar x → need LR T1 (fvar x) ✓ (fvar_LR).
   Else: Gamma y = Some T' → LR T' (sigma y) = LR T' (sigma0 y) ✓.
4. Hbody0 := IH x Hx sigma0 Henv0 : LR T2 (subst sigma0 (open t1 (fvar x))).
5. Rewrite using subst_open_fvar_gen: subst sigma0 (open t1 (fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t1) (fvar x). Requires: ~ fv t1 x, (forall y, lc (sigma y)), lc (fvar x) ✓.
   (forall y, lc (sigma y)): from Henv? Hmm! env_ok Gamma sigma gives LR (Gamma y) (sigma y) only for y in dom Gamma. For y ∉ dom Gamma, sigma y is arbitrary and might not be lc. Hmm. That's a problem: the substitution lemma needs all sigma y lc (at least those appearing in t1).

   Fix: strengthen env_ok to require ALL sigma y to be locally closed:
   Definition env_ok Gamma sigma := (forall y, locally_closed (sigma y)) /\ (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> LR T (sigma x)).
   
   This is fine for the empty-context case (choose sigma = identity → all fvars lc ✓).
   And it's preserved by subst_update: sigma0 y = fvar x or sigma y, both lc ✓.
   
   Also for the T_Abs case where we build sigma' = update sigma x s with s ∈ LR T1 (hence lc) ✓.
   
6. So Hbody0 : LR T2 (open (subst sigma t1) (fvar x)).

   (c) forall s, LR T1 s -> LR T2 (app (abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) s):
       Use the lemma "LR_of_reducts_nonvalue" or the direct proof:
       Let body' := subst sigma t1.
       Given Hs : LR T1 s. Need LR T2 (app (abs T1 body') s).

  normalization: forall t T, empty |-- t \in T -> SN t.

Now, let's also think about whether the relation needs *more* components. Let's re-examine LR's definition:

Definition LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  locally_closed t /\
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => True
  | Ty_Arrow A B => forall s, LR A s -> LR B (tm_app t s)
  end.

Since LR is recursively defined on ty, Rocq will accept this as a Fixpoint returning Prop? The match is on T and the recursive calls are on subterms ✓. But the result type is a function tm -> Prop. We can define:

Fixpoint LR (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow A B => fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\ (forall s, LR A s -> LR B (tm_app t s))
  end.

Structural recursion on T ✓ (LR A and LR B are subterms) ✓.

Let me draft the code:

```coq
(* after strongly_normalizing *)

(* ---------- Fresh atoms and free variables ---------- *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => (x :: nil)%list
  | tm_app t1 t2 => (fv t1 ++ fv t2)%list
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Fixpoint max_list (l : list atom) : nat :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Lemma max_list_ub : forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_list l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| y ys IH]; simpl; intros x Hin.
  - destruct Hin.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH, Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall l, exists x, ~ In x l.
Proof.
  intros l. exists (S (max_list l)). intro H.
  pose proof (max_list_ub l (S (max_list l)) H). lia.
Qed.
```

```coq
(* ---------- Basic properties of lc_at, step, values, SN ---------- *)

Lemma lc_at_mono : forall k k' t, k <= k' -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t.
Proof.
  intros k k' t Hle H. revert k' Hle.
  induction H; intros k' Hle.
  - apply lc_bvar. lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app; auto.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IHlc_at. lia.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma lc_open : forall k t u,
  lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).
Proof.
  intros k t. revert k.
  induction t; intros k u Ht Hu; simpl.
  - (* bvar n *) 
    destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. exact Hu.
    + apply lc_bvar. apply Nat.eqb_neq in E.
      inversion Ht as [k' i Hi]; subst. lia.  (* hmm *)
```

```coq
Lemma inert_no_step : forall t, inert t -> forall t', ~ (t --> t').
```

Now the LR definition:

```coq
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow A B => fun t =>
      locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\ (forall s, LR A s -> LR B (tm_app t s))
  end.
```

Lemmas:
```coq
Lemma LR_lc : forall T t, LR T t -> locally_closed t.
Proof. intros T t H. destruct T; simpl in H; tauto. Qed.

Lemma LR_SN : forall T t, LR T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof. intros T t H. destruct T; simpl in H; tauto. Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma inert_LR : forall T t, inert t -> locally_closed t -> LR T t.

  induction T as [| A IHA B IHB]; intros t Hi Hlc; simpl.
```

```coq
    + apply inert_app, Hi.
    + simpl. apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply LR_lc with (T := A); exact Hs].
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma LR_step : forall T t, LR T t -> forall t', t --> t' -> LR T t'.
```

Now LR_of_reducts_nonvalue:

```coq
Lemma LR_of_reducts : forall T t,
  locally_closed t ->
  strongly_normalizing t ->
  ~ value t ->
  (forall t', t --> t' -> LR T t') ->
  LR T t.
Proof.
  induction T as [| A IHA B IHB]; intros t Hlc Hsn Hnv Hred; simpl.
  - split; assumption.
  - split; [exact Hlc | split; [exact Hsn |]].
    intros s Hs.
    apply IHB with (t := tm_app t s).
    + simpl. apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply LR_lc with (T := A); exact Hs].
    + apply SN_intro. intros r Hr.
      inversion Hr as [T0 body v Hlcabs Hv | t1 t1' t2' Hs1 Hlc2 | v1 t2 t2' Hv Hs2]; subst.
      * (* AppAbs: t = abs T0 body, s = v *) exfalso. apply Hnv. apply v_abs. assumption.
      * (* App1: t --> t1' *) 
        (* need SN (app t1' s) *) 
        apply LR_SN with (T := B). apply (Hred t1'). assumption.
      * (* App2: value t *) exfalso. apply Hnv. assumption.
    + (* ~ value (app t s) *)
      intro Hv. inversion Hv.
    + intros r Hr.
      inversion Hr as [...]; subst.
      * exfalso; apply Hnv; apply v_abs; assumption.
      * apply (Hred t1'); assumption.  (* hmm need LR (app t1' s) *)
      * exfalso; apply Hnv; assumption.
```

Careful with the App1 case: the step is `tm_app t s --> tm_app t1' s` where `t --> t1'`. But in the inversion, the variables might be named differently. The conclusion r = tm_app t1' s (with the App1 rule: t1 --> t1', locally_closed t2, app t1 t2 --> app t1' t2). So t1 = t, t2 = s, r = app t1' s ✓.

We need LR B (app t1' s): from Hred t1' : LR (A→B) t1' (since t --> t1'), and its arrow clause with s ✓: `apply (Hred t1' Hstep)` gives LR (A→B) t1', then we need to extract the arrow clause: destruct it. Since LR (A→B) t1' = lc /\ SN /\ forall s, LR A s -> LR B (app t1' s). So:

```coq
      * apply (proj2 (proj2 (Hred t1' H1)) s Hs).
```
or 
```coq
      * assert (HLR' : LR (Ty_Arrow A B) t1') by (apply Hred; assumption).
        exact (proj2 (proj2 HLR') s Hs).
```
Hmm, proj2 of a triple conjunction: LR (A→B) t1' unfolds (after simpl) to lc /\ (SN /\ (...)) — since `/\` is right-associative: lc /\ SN /\ X parses as lc /\ (SN /\ X). So proj2 gives SN /\ X, proj2 (proj2 ...) gives X ✓. But relying on the unfolding requires simpl to expose it. Alternatively write a helper lemma:

```coq
Lemma LR_app : forall A B t s, LR (Ty_Arrow A B) t -> LR A s -> LR B (tm_app t s).
Proof. intros A B t s H Hs. simpl in H. tauto. Qed.
```
Hmm, `simpl in H` then H : lc /\ SN /\ (forall s, LR A s -> LR B (app t s)); tauto might work but let's do `destruct H as [? [? H]]; apply H; assumption` ✓.

Similarly for SN at arrow: `LR_SN` handles it ✓.

Now the main lemma LR_abs_app. Let me write it:

```coq
Lemma LR_abs_app : forall s,
  strongly_normalizing s ->
  forall A body B,
    locally_closed (tm_abs A body) ->
    (forall s', LR A s' -> LR B (open body s')) ->
    LR A s ->
    LR B (tm_app (tm_abs A body) s).
Proof.
  intros s Hsn. induction Hsn as [s Hred IH].
  intros A body B Hlcabs Hopen HAs.
  (* show LR B (app (abs A body) s) *)
  ...
Qed.
```

Proof strategy:
1. First establish LR (A→B)?? No. We need LR B (app abs s).

Let me define a helper: prove `Hsn_app : strongly_normalizing (tm_app (tm_abs A body) s)` first:
```coq
  assert (Hsn_app : strongly_normalizing (tm_app (tm_abs A body) s)).
  { apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep.
    inversion Hstep as [T0 body0 v Hlc0 Hv | t1 t1' t2 Hstep1 Hlc2 | v1 t2 t2' Hv Hstep2]; subst.
    - (* AppAbs: t' = open body s (with abs A body = abs T0 body0, s = v) *)
      apply LR_SN with (T := B). apply Hopen. exact HAs.
    - (* App1: abs A body --> t1' *) 
      exfalso. apply (step_abs_false A body t1'). assumption.
    - (* App2: t' = app (abs A body) t2', s --> t2' *)
      (* s = t2? Wait: App2 is app v1 t2 --> app v1 t2' with v1 = abs A body, t2 = s *)
      apply LR_SN with (T := B). 
      apply IH with (A := A) (body := body) (B := B); auto.
      * (* LR A t2' from LR A s *) 
        eapply LR_step; eassumption.
  }
```
Wait, the IH here: from `induction Hsn as [s Hred IH]` with motive P s := forall A body B, lc -> hopen -> LR A s -> LR B (app abs s). So IH : forall s', s --> s' -> P s'. So `apply IH with (A:=A)(body:=body)(B:=B)` needs: s --> t2' ✓, lc abs ✓, hopen ✓, LR A t2' ✓. Hmm, the order of arguments. I'll write `apply (IH t2' Hstep2 A body B Hlcabs Hopen ...)`.

Hmm wait, but the motive P s must be the entire goal after generalizing: `forall A body B, ...`. Since I wrote `intros s Hsn. induction Hsn as [s Hred IH]. intros A body B Hlcabs Hopen HAs.`, the motive is fun s0 => (forall A body B, lc(abs A body) -> (forall s', LR A s' -> LR B (open body s')) -> LR A s0 -> LR B (app (abs A body) s0)) ✓. 2. Then prove LR B (app abs s):
```coq

    inversion Hstep as [...]; subst.

    + (* App1 *) exfalso. eapply step_abs_false; eassumption.
```

That's the structure. Note: we need `LR_of_reducts` at B with the term app abs s. And in the SN proof we need `LR_SN` of the reducts ✓.

Hold on, there's an issue with the SN proof in step 1: for the App2 case, I used IH to get LR B (app abs s') then LR_SN ✓. And for AppAbs, LR B (open body s) from Hopen ✓.

But careful: `inversion Hstep` where Hstep : tm_app (tm_abs A body) s --> t'. The AppAbs case: ST_AppAbs : forall T t v, lc (abs T t) -> value v -> app (abs T t) v --> open t v. Also gives hypotheses lc(abs A body) (we already have) and value s.

Next: the substitution machinery.

```coq
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition subst_update (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (v : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then v else sigma y.

Lemma subst_update_same : forall sigma x v, subst_update sigma x v x = v.
Proof. intros. unfold subst_update. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma subst_update_diff : forall sigma x v y, x <> y -> subst_update sigma x v y = sigma y.
Proof. intros. unfold subst_update. apply Nat.eqb_neq in H. rewrite H. reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma subst_id : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t.
Proof. induction t; simpl; congruence. Qed.
```
Hmm, `induction t; simpl; congruence` — for app/abs, `f_equal; assumption`. Let's do `induction t; simpl; try reflexivity; try (f_equal; assumption)`. Actually `congruence` might handle it. Let's write:

```coq
Lemma subst_id : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t.
Proof.
  induction t; simpl; try reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt1, IHt2. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt. reflexivity.
Qed.
```
Note constructor order: tm_bvar, tm_fvar, tm_app, tm_abs, tm_true, tm_false. The `induction t` gives 6 goals; the names IHt1, IHt2, IHt. Let me write `induction t as [i | x | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]; simpl`.

Now the key commutation lemma:

```coq
Lemma subst_open_gen : forall t k x sigma v,
  ~ In x (fv t) ->
  (forall y, locally_closed (sigma y)) ->
  locally_closed v ->
  subst (subst_update sigma x v) (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t)
  = open_rec k v (subst sigma t).

  induction t as [i | y | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]; intros k x sigma v Hfresh Hlcv Hlc; simpl.
```

- (* fvar y *) 
  Need: subst (subst_update sigma x v) (fvar y) = open_rec k v (sigma y).

Since all bvars in t are < their binder depth ≤ ... hmm, lc_at 0 t means: bvar i occurring at depth d has i < d. open_rec k at depth d replaces bvar k. If k < d then replacement happens; but can bvar k occur at depth d > k? Yes! E.g., t = abs T (bvar 0): lc_at 0 ✓ (0 < 1), and open_rec 1 u t: at depth 1 inside the abs, replaces bvar 1 — not present. Try t = abs T (abs T' (bvar 1)): lc_at 0 requires 1 < 2 ✓; open_rec 1 u t → at the inner abs, k becomes 2 → no replacement of bvar 1; open_rec 0 similarly... Let me think: if t is locally closed, can an occurrence of bvar k appear at a depth where open_rec k u t replaces it? open_rec k u t replaces bvar k occurrences at binder depth d with the current index k+d. An occurrence of bvar i at depth d satisfies i < d (lc). The replacement happens if i = k + d, i.e., d + k = i < d → k < 0, impossible ✓. So yes: locally_closed t → open_rec k u t = t ✓. 
  
  Wait, but also the traversal increases k under binders; the condition is i = k + d where d is the number of binders traversed from the root. lc_at 0 gives i < d. Since k + d ≥ d > i, no match ✓.
  
  Let me prove: Lemma lc_open_rec_id : forall t k u, locally_closed t -> open_rec k u t = t.

Hmm, but that's not quite right either: lc_at k t means bvars < k + depth? Hmm, lc_at k t is defined as: bvar i at depth d (under the binder count relative to the root) satisfies i < k + d. Then open_rec k u t replaces bvar (k+d) at depth d; since i < k + d, no replacement ✓. So the generalized statement:
  
  Lemma lc_open_rec_id_gen : forall t k n u, lc_at n t -> open_rec k u t = t? 
  
  Hmm, the index relation: open_rec starts at k, increments under binders. lc_at starts at n. If n = k, we get: occurrences at depth d satisfy i < k + d, and replacement targets k + d ✓ no match. So:
  
  Lemma lc_open_rec_id : forall t k u, lc_at k t -> open_rec k u t = t.
  Proof by induction on t, generalizing k:
    - bvar i: lc_at k (bvar i) → i < k. open_rec k u (bvar i) = if eqb k i then u else bvar i. Since i < k, i ≠ k → bvar i ✓.
    - fvar ✓, true/false ✓.

  ✓
  
  Great, so `lc_open_rec_id : forall t k u, lc_at k t -> open_rec k u t = t`. And locally_closed = lc_at 0, so with k=0 ✓.

- app case: LHS = subst ... (app (open_rec k fx t1) (open_rec k fx t2)) = app (subst ... (open_rec k fx t1)) (subst ... The freshness condition for subterms: ~ In x (fv t) → ~ In x (fv t1) ✓ (fv (app t1 t2) = fv t1 ++ fv t2).

- abs: LHS = subst ... (abs T (open_rec (S k) fx t1)) = abs T (subst ... (open_rec (S k) fx t1)); RHS = abs T (open_rec (S k) v (subst sigma t1)). Apply IH with k := S k ✓.

Good.

Also, the `~ In x (fv t)` condition: I used `fv : tm -> list atom`. Let me define fv as a list:

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar y => y :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true | tm_false => nil
  end.

In the fvar case, Hfresh : ~ In y (y :: nil) → y ≠ x? ~ In x (fv (fvar y)) = ~ In x (y :: nil) → x ≠ y ✓.

Now the fundamental theorem:

```coq
Definition subst_lc (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall y, locally_closed (sigma y).

Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  subst_lc sigma /\ (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> LR T (sigma x)).

Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR T (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H.
  induction H as [Gamma x T Hx
                | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHbody
                | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
                | Gamma | Gamma].
  ...
```

Hmm, the constructor argument names. Let me check each:
- T_Var : forall Gamma x T, Gamma x = Some T -> has_type Gamma (fvar x) T.
- T_Abs : forall (L : list atom) Gamma T1 T2 t1, (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2) -> has_type Gamma (abs T1 t1) (T1→T2).
- T_App : forall Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2, has_type Gamma t1 (T1→T2) -> has_type Gamma t2 T1 -> has_type Gamma (app t1 t2) T2.
- T_True : forall Gamma, has_type Gamma true Bool.
- T_False.

The induction will give IH for T_Abs's premise. I'm not 100% sure how Coq handles the nested forall. Let's test.

If we do `induction H` with Gamma, t, T not generalized... the goal depends on Gamma, t, T which are the indices of H. Coq's induction will abstract over them and require the motive to be well-formed; since the goal is a function of Gamma, t, T ✓, it works. The IHs will be `forall sigma, env_ok Gamma' sigma -> LR T' (subst sigma t')` for the sub-derivations ✓.

Then the final theorem:
```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T H.
  assert (Henv : env_ok empty (fun x => tm_fvar x)).
  { split.
    - intros y. apply lc_fvar.
    - intros x T' Hx. simpl in Hx. discriminate. }
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T H (fun x => tm_fvar x) Henv) as HLR.
  rewrite subst_id in HLR.
  apply LR_SN with (T := T). exact HLR.
Qed.
```

Let me now also double check `LR_of_reducts`'s statement ordering: I need the induction on T with the IH for B. Let me write:

```coq
Lemma LR_of_reducts : forall T t,
  locally_closed t ->
  strongly_normalizing t ->
  ~ value t ->
  (forall t', t --> t' -> LR T t') ->
  LR T t.
Proof.
  induction T as [| A IHA B IHB]; intros t Hlc Hsn Hnv Hred; simpl.
  - split; assumption.
  - split; [assumption | split; [assumption |]].
    intros s Hs.
    apply IHB.
    + simpl. apply lc_app; [assumption | apply LR_lc with (T := A); assumption].
    + apply SN_intro. intros r Hr.
      inversion Hr; subst.
      * (* AppAbs *) exfalso. apply Hnv. apply v_abs. assumption.
      * (* App1 *) apply LR_SN with (T := B). apply Hred. assumption.
      * (* App2 *) exfalso. apply Hnv. assumption.
    + intro Hv. inversion Hv.
    + intros r Hr. inversion Hr; subst.
      * exfalso. apply Hnv. apply v_abs. assumption.
      * apply Hred. assumption.
      * exfalso. apply Hnv. assumption.
Qed.
```

Hmm, careful: in the App1 case, `Hred t1' H` gives LR (A→B) t1' — I need to extract the app clause. Let me write:

```coq
      * (* App1 *) 
        assert (HLR' : LR (Ty_Arrow A B) t1') by (apply Hred; assumption).
        apply LR_SN with (T := B).
        (* need SN (app t1' s) *) 
        apply LR_app with (A := A); assumption.
```
Using the helper LR_app: LR (Arrow A B) t -> LR A s -> LR B (app t s) ✓.

```coq
Lemma LR_abs_app : forall s,
  strongly_normalizing s ->
  forall A body B,
    locally_closed (tm_abs A body) ->
    (forall s', LR A s' -> LR B (open body s')) ->
    LR A s ->
    LR B (tm_app (tm_abs A body) s).
Proof.
  intros s Hsn. induction Hsn as [s Hred IH].
  intros A body B Hlcabs Hopen HAs.
  assert (Hsn_app : strongly_normalizing (tm_app (tm_abs A body) s)).
  { apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep.
    inversion Hstep; subst.
    - (* AppAbs *) apply LR_SN with (T := B). apply Hopen. exact HAs.
    - (* App1 *) exfalso. eapply step_abs_false. eexact H0.  (* name *)
    - (* App2 *) 
      apply LR_SN with (T := B).
      apply (IH t2' H0 A body B Hlcabs Hopen).
      eapply LR_step; eassumption.
  }
  apply LR_of_reducts with (t := tm_app (tm_abs A body) s).
  - simpl. apply lc_app; [exact Hlcabs | apply LR_lc with (T := A); exact HAs].
  - exact Hsn_app.
  - intro Hv. inversion Hv.
  - intros t' Hstep. inversion Hstep; subst.
    + apply Hopen. exact HAs.
    + exfalso. eapply step_abs_false. eexact H0.
    + apply (IH t2' H0 A body B Hlcabs Hopen). eapply LR_step; eassumption.
Qed.
```

```coq
(* ============================================================ *)
(* Logical relation for strong normalization                    *)
(* ============================================================ *)

(* ---------- Free variables and freshness ---------- *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Fixpoint max_list (l : list atom) : nat :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Lemma max_list_ub : forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_list l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| y ys IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - destruct Hin.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; eassumption | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall l : list atom, exists x, ~ In x l.
Proof.
  intros l. exists (S (max_list l)). intro Hin.
  pose proof (max_list_ub l _ Hin). lia.
Qed.
```

Careful: `destruct Hin as [-> | Hin]` on `In x (y :: ys)` — In is defined as `x = y \/ In x ys`; so `[-> | Hin]` gives x = y in the first case... `->` pattern substitutes x := y? The equation is `x = y`, so `->` replaces x by y ✓ (goal `x <= max x (max_list ys)` becomes `y <= ...` ✓).

```coq
(* Auxiliary syntactic notions                                      *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2

  - destruct Hin.
```

```coq
Lemma lc_open : forall (t : tm) k u,
  lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).
Proof.
  induction t as [n | x | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]; intros k u Ht Hu; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. exact Hu.
    + apply lc_bvar. apply Nat.eqb_neq in E.
      inversion Ht as [k0 i Hi]; subst. lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app; [apply IH1 | apply IH2]; assumption.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IH.
    + inversion Ht as [k0 T0 t0 H0]; subst. exact H0.
    + apply lc_at_mono with (k := k); [lia | exact Hu].
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.
```

Hmm, `inversion Ht as [k0 i Hi]; subst` — inversion on `lc_at (S k) (tm_bvar n)` gives the lc_bvar constructor with k0 = S k, i = n, and Hi : n < S k. With `subst`, the goal `lc_at k (tm_bvar n)` and we need n < k; from n < S k and n ≠ k → lia ✓.

```coq
Lemma lc_open_inv : forall (t : tm) k u,
  lc_at k (open_rec k u t) -> lc_at 0 u -> lc_at (S k) t.
```

```coq
Lemma lc_open_rec_id : forall (t : tm) k u, lc_at k t -> open_rec k u t = t.

  - reflexivity.
```
  - reflexivity.
Qed.

For the subst_open_gen lemma, I need the fv function to compute over tm. Let me write:

```coq
Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.
```

```coq
(* ================================================================ *)
(* Logical relation for strong normalization                        *)
(* ================================================================ *)

(* ---------------- Free variables and freshness ---------------- *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Fixpoint max_list (l : list atom) : nat :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Lemma max_list_ub : forall (l : list atom) x, In x l -> x <= max_list l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| y ys IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - destruct Hin.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; eassumption | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall l : list atom, exists x : atom, ~ In x l.
Proof.
  intros l. exists (S (max_list l)). intro Hin.
  pose proof (max_list_ub l _ Hin). lia.
Qed.

(* ---------------- Local closure lemmas ---------------- *)

Lemma lc_at_mono : forall (t : tm) k k', k <= k' -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t.
Proof.
  intros t k k' Hle H. revert k' Hle.
  induction H as [k i Hi | k x | k t1 t2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | k T t1 H1 IH1 | k | k];
    intros k' Hle.
  - apply lc_bvar. lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app; assumption.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IH1. lia.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.
```

```coq
(* ================================================================ *)
(* Strong normalization via a unary logical relation                *)
(* ================================================================ *)

(* ---------------- Free variables and freshness ---------------- *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Fixpoint max_list (l : list atom) : nat :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Lemma max_list_ub : forall (l : list atom) x, In x l -> x <= max_list l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| y ys IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - destruct Hin.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; eassumption | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall l : list atom, exists x : atom, ~ In x l.
Proof.
  intros l. exists (S (max_list l)). intro Hin.
  pose proof (max_list_ub l _ Hin). lia.
Qed.

(* ---------------- Local closure ---------------- *)

Lemma lc_at_mono : forall (t : tm) k k', k <= k' -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t.
Proof.
  intros t k k' Hle H. revert k' Hle.
  induction H as [k i Hi | k x | k t1 t2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | k T t1 H1 IH1 | k | k];
    intros k' Hle.
  - apply lc_bvar. lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app; assumption.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IH1. lia.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.
```

Let me write `apply IH1; assumption` — hmm, `apply IH1` will unify the conclusion `lc_at k0 t1` with `lc_at k' t1`, leaving `k <= k'` which is Hle ✓ assumption. Actually, one concern with `induction H as [k i Hi | ...]` for lc_at: since lc_at is indexed by k and t, the induction principle lc_at_ind has P : nat -> tm -> Prop and the constructor cases as printed:
  f : forall k i, i < k -> P k (bvar i)
  f0 : forall k x, P k (fvar x)
  f1 : forall k t1 t2, lc_at k t1 -> P k t1 -> lc_at k t2 -> P k t2 -> P k (app t1 t2)
  f2 : forall k T t1, lc_at (S k) t1 -> P (S k) t1 -> P k (abs T t1)
  f3 : forall k, P k true
  f4 : forall k, P k false

```coq
(* ================================================================ *)
(* Development for strong normalization                             *)
(* ================================================================ *)

(* ---------------- Free variables and freshness ---------------- *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Fixpoint max_list (l : list atom) : nat :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Lemma max_list_ub : forall (l : list atom) x, In x l -> x <= max_list l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| y ys IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - destruct Hin.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; eassumption | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall l : list atom, exists x : atom, ~ In x l.
Proof.
  intros l. exists (S (max_list l)). intro Hin.
  pose proof (max_list_ub l _ Hin). lia.
Qed.

(* ---------------- Local closure lemmas ---------------- *)

Lemma lc_at_mono : forall (t : tm) k k', k <= k' -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t.
Proof.
  intros t k k' Hle H. revert k' Hle.
  induction H as [k i Hi | k x | k t1 t2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | k T t1 H1 IH1 | k | k];
    intros k' Hle.
  - apply lc_bvar. lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app.
    + apply IH1. assumption.
    + apply IH2. assumption.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IH1. lia.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.

Lemma lc_open : forall (t : tm) k u,
  lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).
Proof.
  induction t as [n | x | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]; intros k u Ht Hu; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst n. exact Hu.
    + apply lc_bvar. apply Nat.eqb_neq in E.
      inversion Ht as [k0 i Hi]; subst. lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app.
    + apply IH1; assumption.
    + apply IH2; assumption.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IH.
    + inversion Ht as [k0 T0 t0 H0]; subst. exact H0.
    + apply lc_at_mono with (k := k); [lia | exact Hu].
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.

Lemma lc_open_inv : forall (t : tm) k u,
  lc_at k (open_rec k u t) -> lc_at 0 u -> lc_at (S k) t.
Proof.
  induction t as [n | x | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]; intros k u H Hlu; simpl in *.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst n. apply lc_bvar. lia.
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. apply lc_bvar.
      inversion H as [k0 i Hi]; subst. lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app.
    + apply IH1 with (u := u); assumption.
    + apply IH2 with (u := u); assumption.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IH with (u := u).
    + inversion H as [k0 T0 t0 H0]; subst. exact H0.
    + exact Hlu.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.

Lemma lc_open_rec_id : forall (t : tm) k u, lc_at k t -> open_rec k u t = t.
Proof.
  induction t as [n | x | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]; intros k u H; simpl in *.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst n. inversion H; lia.
    + reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - inversion H as [k0 a b Ha Hb]; subst. rewrite IH1, IH2; assumption.
  - rewrite IH.
    + reflexivity.
    + inversion H as [k0 T0 t0 H0]; subst. exact H0.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.
```

Careful with `rewrite IH1, IH2; assumption` — the goals after rewrite: `open_rec k u t1 = t1` → hmm, `rewrite IH1` rewrites occurrences of `open_rec k u t1` in the goal to `t1`, but the goal is an equality `tm_app (open_rec k u t1) (open_rec k u t2) = tm_app t1 t2`. Rewriting with IH1 requires proving its premises first: `lc_at k t1` — rewrite generates subgoals after. Let me restructure:

```coq
  - inversion H as [k0 a b Ha Hb]; subst.
    rewrite (IH1 k u Ha), (IH2 k u Hb). reflexivity.
```
✓ cleaner.

Similarly for the abs case: `rewrite (IH (S k) u H0)`. Hmm, IH : forall k u, lc_at k t1 -> open_rec k u t1 = t1. So `rewrite (IH (S k) u H0)` ✓.

```coq
  - intros Hv. inversion Hv.
```

```coq
Lemma inert_no_step : forall t, inert t -> forall t', ~ (t --> t').
Proof.
  intros t Hi. induction Hi as [x | i | t1 t2 Hi IH].
  - intros t' Hstep. inversion Hstep.
  - intros t' Hstep. inversion Hstep.
  - intros t' Hstep. inversion Hstep; subst.
    + (* AppAbs *) inversion Hi.
    + (* App1 *) apply IH. eassumption.
    + (* App2 *) eapply inert_not_value; eassumption.
Qed.
```
This relies on the ordering of the three inversion subgoals being AppAbs, App1, App2 (matching the constructor order) ✓ — that's how inversion orders them.

Now the LR:

```coq
(* ---------------- The logical relation ---------------- *)

Fixpoint LR (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow A B =>
      fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
               (forall s, LR A s -> LR B (tm_app t s))
  end.

Lemma LR_lc : forall T t, LR T t -> locally_closed t.
Proof.
  intros T t H. destruct T as [| A B]; simpl in H.
  - apply H.
  - apply H.
Qed.

Lemma LR_SN : forall T t, LR T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros T t H. destruct T as [| A B]; simpl in H.
  - apply H.
  - apply (proj1 (proj2 H)).
Qed.

Lemma LR_app : forall A B t s, LR (Ty_Arrow A B) t -> LR A s -> LR B (tm_app t s).
Proof.
  intros A B t s H Hs. simpl in H.
  apply (proj2 (proj2 H)). exact Hs.
Qed.
```

Hmm, `Lemma LR_lc` with `destruct T as [| A B]; simpl in H. - apply H.` — in the Bool case, H after simpl : locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t; goal locally_closed t; `apply H` ✓ (apply works for conjunctions? `apply H` where H : A /\ B and goal A — yes, Coq's apply can use a conjunction as a lemma with multiple premises? Hmm, `apply H` with H : A /\ B to prove A: Coq will use the "apply with a conjunction" trick — I believe `apply H` works when H is a conjunction containing the goal as the first component? Actually, `apply` uses the conclusion of H (up to reduction); A /\ B is an inductive with one constructor `conj : A -> B -> A /\ B`. The conclusion of H as a lemma is `A /\ B` which doesn't directly unify with A. Coq's `apply` does a "head reduction" of H's type: for a hypothesis of type `A /\ B`, `apply` will not automatically destruct. Hmm, actually Coq's apply does handle it: `apply H` where H : A /\ B and goal A — I believe this works because apply can use the projections? Let me recall: In Coq, `apply` on a hypothesis whose type is an inductive with one constructor will try to unify the *conclusion*; for `A /\ B` the conclusion is `A /\ B`, which doesn't unify with A. But there's a special case: "apply" tries `constructor`-like behavior? I recall `apply H` where H : A /\ B does work for goal A in recent Coq versions? Hmm, I'm not sure. Safer: `destruct H as [Ha Hb]; exact Ha`. Let me write explicit destructs.


Now LR_of_reducts:

```coq
Lemma LR_of_reducts : forall T t,
  locally_closed t ->
  strongly_normalizing t ->
  ~ value t ->
  (forall t', t --> t' -> LR T t') ->
  LR T t.
Proof.
  induction T as [| A IHA B IHB]; intros t Hlc Hsn Hnv Hred; simpl.
  - split; assumption.
  - split; [exact Hlc | split; [exact Hsn |]].
    intros s Hs.
    apply IHB with (t := tm_app t s).
    + simpl. apply lc_app.
      * exact Hlc.
      * apply LR_lc with (T := A). exact Hs.
    + apply SN_intro. intros r Hr. inversion Hr; subst.
      * exfalso. apply Hnv. apply v_abs. assumption.
      * apply LR_SN with (T := B). apply LR_app with (A := A).
        -- apply Hred. eassumption.
        -- exact Hs.
      * exfalso. apply Hnv. assumption.
    + intro Hv. apply value_app_false in Hv. exact Hv.
    + intros r Hr. inversion Hr; subst.
      * exfalso. apply Hnv. apply v_abs. assumption.
      * apply LR_app with (A := A).
        -- apply Hred. eassumption.
        -- exact Hs.
      * exfalso. apply Hnv. assumption.
Qed.
```

Hmm, careful with the inversion naming and the hypothesis ordering. Let me reconsider the ST_App1 inversion case: the step is `tm_app t s --> r`, rule ST_App1 with premises `t --> t1'` and `locally_closed s`; the conclusion r = `tm_app t1' s`. So in the goal `LR B r` after subst r := app t1' s, we need LR B (app t1' s) where t --> t1'. `apply LR_app with (A := A)` gives LR (A→B) t1' and LR A s; `apply Hred; eassumption` gives LR (A→B) t1' ✓.

For the AppAbs case: the step is `tm_app (tm_abs T0 body) v --> open body v` where t = tm_abs T0 body, s = v — we get `locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body)` and value v; contradict Hnv via v_abs ✓.

For App2: `value t` contradicts Hnv ✓.

Ordering of inversion subgoals = constructor order: AppAbs, App1, App2 ✓.

But caution: `inversion Hr; subst` where Hr : tm_app t s --> r. Will inversion produce the App1 subgoal with a hypothesis of type `t --> t1'`? Yes.

Now `value_app_false` usage: `intro Hv. apply value_app_false in Hv. exact Hv.` — value_app_false : forall t1 t2, ~ value (app t1 t2). Applying it to Hv : value (app t s) gives False ✓. Or simply `intro Hv; exact (value_app_false t s Hv)`.

Also `simpl` on the goal `lc_at 0 (tm_app t s)`: `simpl` might not change; then `apply lc_app` ✓.

Now the big one, LR_abs_app:

```coq
Lemma LR_abs_app : forall s,
  strongly_normalizing s ->
  forall (A : ty) (body : tm) (B : ty),
    locally_closed (tm_abs A body) ->
    (forall s', LR A s' -> LR B (open body s')) ->
    LR A s ->
    LR B (tm_app (tm_abs A body) s).
Proof.
  intros s Hsn. induction Hsn as [s Hred IHred].
  intros A body B Hlcabs Hopen HAs.
  assert (Hsn_app : strongly_normalizing (tm_app (tm_abs A body) s)).
  { apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep. inversion Hstep; subst.
    - apply LR_SN with (T := B). apply Hopen. exact HAs.
    - exfalso. eapply step_abs_false. eassumption.
    - apply LR_SN with (T := B).
      apply (IHred t2 H0 A body B Hlcabs Hopen).
      eapply LR_step; eassumption.
  }
  apply LR_of_reducts with (t := tm_app (tm_abs A body) s).
  - simpl. apply lc_app.
    + exact Hlcabs.
    + apply LR_lc with (T := A). exact HAs.
  - exact Hsn_app.
  - intro Hv. exact (value_app_false _ _ Hv).
  - intros t' Hstep. inversion Hstep; subst.
    + apply Hopen. exact HAs.
    + exfalso. eapply step_abs_false. eassumption.
    + apply (IHred t2 H0 A body B Hlcabs Hopen).
      eapply LR_step; eassumption.
Qed.
```

The problematic bit is `H0` — the name of the step hypothesis `s --> t2` in the App2 inversion case. Since I destruct Hstep with inversion and there are multiple hypotheses, Coq will name them H, H0, H1... Predictably: in the ST_AppAbst case, hypotheses are named H and H0 maybe; in each subgoal the context has its own names. This is fragile. Better to write a custom inversion lemma:

```coq
Lemma step_app_abs_inv : forall (A : ty) (body s t' : tm),
  tm_app (tm_abs A body) s --> t' ->
  (value s /\ t' = open body s) \/
  (exists s', s --> s' /\ t' = tm_app (tm_abs A body) s').
Proof.
  intros A body s t' H. inversion H; subst.
  - left. split; [assumption | reflexivity].
  - exfalso. eapply step_abs_false. eassumption.
  - right. eexists. split; [eassumption | reflexivity].
Qed.
```

Hmm, the AppAbs case: after inversion and subst, the goal is `(value s /\ open body s = open body s) \/ ...` hmm. Let me think about how inversion handles this. H : tm_app (tm_abs A body) s --> t'. ST_AppAbs's conclusion: tm_app (tm_abs T t) v --> open t v. Unification: T := A, t := body, v := s, t' := open body s. Hypotheses: locally_closed (tm_abs A body) and value s. After `subst`, the goal becomes `(value s /\ open body s = open body s) \/ (exists s', s --> s' /\ open body s = tm_app (tm_abs A body) s')`. Then `left; split; [assumption | reflexivity]` ✓.

ST_App1's conclusion: tm_app t1 t2 --> tm_app t1' t2, with t1 --> t1' and lc t2. Unify t1 := tm_abs A body, t2 := s, t' := tm_app t1' s. Hypothesis: tm_abs A body --> t1' — impossible. So the goal is the disjunction; we need exfalso with step_abs_false ✓.

ST_App2: conclusion tm_app v1 t2 --> tm_app v1 t2' with value v1, t2 --> t2'. Unify v1 := tm_abs A body, t2 := s, t2' := s', t' := tm_app (tm_abs A body) s'. Goal: `(value s /\ ...) \/ (exists s', s --> s' /\ tm_app (tm_abs A body) s' = tm_app (tm_abs A body) s')`. Right; eexists; split; [the step | reflexivity] ✓.

Hmm, in the App2 case, the existential variable name: `eexists` introduces `?s'`; split; first goal `s --> ?s'` solved by eassumption (the hypothesis s --> t2') — but we need the witness to be t2'. `eexists` picks an evar, then eassumption unifies ✓. Second goal: `tm_app (tm_abs A body) ?s' = tm_app (tm_abs A body) t2'` → after eassumption unifies ?s' := t2', reflexivity ✓.

But wait: in the ST_App2 case, is t2' a distinct variable from s? Yes.

Now this custom lemma replaces the fragile inversions in LR_abs_app. Usage:

```coq
  assert (Hsn_app : strongly_normalizing (tm_app (tm_abs A body) s)).
  { apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep.
    destruct (step_app_abs_inv A body s t' Hstep) as [[Hv Heq] | [s' [Hs' Heq]]].
    - subst t'. apply LR_SN with (T := B). apply Hopen. exact HAs.
    - subst t'. apply LR_SN with (T := B).
      apply (IHred s' Hs' A body B Hlcabs Hopen).
      eapply LR_step; eassumption.
  }
  apply LR_of_reducts with (t := tm_app (tm_abs A body) s).
  - simpl. apply lc_app; [exact Hlcabs | apply LR_lc with (T := A); exact HAs].
  - exact Hsn_app.
  - intro Hv. exact (value_app_false _ _ Hv).
  - intros t' Hstep.
    destruct (step_app_abs_inv A body s t' Hstep) as [[Hv Heq] | [s' [Hs' Heq]]].
    + subst t'. apply Hopen. exact HAs.
    + subst t'. apply (IHred s' Hs' A body B Hlcabs Hopen).
      eapply LR_step; eassumption.
Qed.
```

Much more robust ✓.

Note: in the AppAbs case, the step `tm_app (abs A body) s --> open body s` requires lc (abs A body) and value s; our step_app_abs_inv gives value s ✓ and t' = open body s ✓.

Hmm, in the SN sub-proof the AppAbs reduct is `open body s` and we need SN of it: LR_SN (Hopen HAs) ✓. In the LR sub-proof: LR B (open body s) ✓.

Great.

Now, the substitution section:

```coq
(* ---------------- Simultaneous substitution ---------------- *)

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition subst_update (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (v : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then v else sigma y.

Lemma subst_update_same : forall sigma x v, subst_update sigma x v x = v.
Proof.
  intros. unfold subst_update. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity.
Qed.

Lemma subst_update_diff : forall sigma x v y, x <> y -> subst_update sigma x v y = sigma y.
Proof.
  intros. unfold subst_update. apply Nat.eqb_neq in H. rewrite H. reflexivity.
Qed.

Lemma subst_id : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t.
Proof.
  induction t as [i | x | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]; simpl.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH1, IH2. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH. reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.
```

Careful: `Nat.eqb_neq` direction. `Nat.eqb_neq : forall n m, (n =? m) = false <-> n <> m`. `apply Nat.eqb_neq in H` where H : x <> y — hmm, `apply ... in H` needs H's statement to match the conclusion of the iff. The iff's forward direction: (x =? y) = false -> x <> y. Backward: x <> y -> (x =? y) = false. `apply Nat.eqb_neq in H` uses the *backward* direction? `apply L in H` where L : A <-> B and H : A gives H : B; where H : B gives H : A. Here H : x <> y = B, so applying gives H : (x =? y) = false ✓. Good.

Actually, I recall `Nat.eqb_neq : forall n m : nat, (n =? m) = false <-> n <> m` ✓.

Then subst_open_gen:

```coq
Lemma subst_open_gen : forall (t : tm) k x sigma v,
  ~ In x (fv t) ->
  (forall y, locally_closed (sigma y)) ->
  locally_closed v ->
  subst (subst_update sigma x v) (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t)
  = open_rec k v (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  induction t as [n | y | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]; intros k x sigma v Hfresh Hslc Hv; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst n. apply subst_update_same.
    + reflexivity.
  - simpl. (* subst_update sigma x v y *)
    assert (Hxy : x <> y).
    { intro Heq. apply Hfresh. subst y. simpl. left. reflexivity. }
    rewrite subst_update_diff; [| exact Hxy].
    symmetry. apply lc_open_rec_id. apply Hslc.
  - rewrite IH1, IH2; [reflexivity | | ]; assumption.
  - f_equal. apply IH.
    + simpl in Hfresh. exact Hfresh.
    + exact Hslc.
    + exact Hv.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.
```

Hmm, several details:
- In the bvar case: LHS = subst (subst_update sigma x v) (if eqb k n then fvar x else bvar n). After destruct with eqn:E, the if in the goal is reduced. In the true branch, the term is `tm_fvar x` and subst gives `subst_update sigma x v x` = v; RHS = open_rec k v (subst sigma (bvar n)) = open_rec k v (bvar n) = if eqb k n then v else bvar n = v ✓. But after `simpl`, did the RHS's `open_rec k v (bvar n)` also get the if? `simpl` unfolds open_rec: `if Nat.eqb k n then v else tm_bvar n`. Then `destruct (Nat.eqb k n)` splits both the LHS and RHS ifs ✓. So in the true branch the goal becomes `v = v` after the subst_update_same rewrite; maybe even `reflexivity`. Let me write:
```coq
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst n. simpl. rewrite subst_update_same. reflexivity.
    + reflexivity.
```
Hmm, careful: after `destruct`, the goal in the true branch: `subst (subst_update sigma x v) (tm_fvar x) = v` → simpl → `subst_update sigma x v x = v` → `apply subst_update_same` ✓. In the false branch: `subst ... (tm_bvar n) = tm_bvar n` → simpl → `tm_bvar n = tm_bvar n` → reflexivity ✓.

Wait, but `simpl` was already applied at the intros line (`intros ... ; simpl`). So the goal after simpl is:
`subst (subst_update sigma x v) (if Nat.eqb k n then tm_fvar x else tm_bvar n) = (if Nat.eqb k n then v else tm_bvar n)`.
Then destruct → true: `subst (subst_update sigma x v) (tm_fvar x) = v`; simpl → `subst_update sigma x v x = v` ✓; apply subst_update_same.
false: `subst (subst_update sigma x v) (tm_bvar n) = tm_bvar n`; simpl → reflexivity ✓.

- fvar case: goal after simpl: `subst (subst_update sigma x v) (tm_fvar y) = open_rec k v (sigma y)` → simpl LHS: `subst_update sigma x v y`. So goal: `subst_update sigma x v y = open_rec k v (sigma y)`. Rewrite with subst_update_diff (needs x <> y) → `sigma y = open_rec k v (sigma y)`. Hslc gives locally_closed (sigma y) = lc_at 0 (sigma y), but we need lc_at k (sigma y)! Use lc_at_mono: 0 <= k ✓. So: `symmetry. apply lc_open_rec_id. apply lc_at_mono with (k := 0); [lia | apply Hslc].`

Hmm, actually: is `locally_closed (sigma y)` = `lc_at 0 (sigma y)` enough for `open_rec k v (sigma y) = sigma y` for arbitrary k? Yes as argued (no bvars at all). But the lemma lc_open_rec_id requires lc_at k. So use monotonicity ✓.

- app case: after simpl, goal: `tm_app (subst ... (open_rec k (fvar x) t1)) (subst ... (open_rec k (fvar x) t2)) = tm_app (open_rec k v (subst sigma t1)) (open_rec k v (subst sigma t2))`. Rewrite IH1, IH2 ✓. The freshness premise: ~ In x (fv (app t1 t2)) = ~ In x (fv t1 ++ fv t2) → both ✓ via in_app_or / in_or_app. I wrote `rewrite IH1, IH2; [reflexivity | | ]; assumption.` Hmm, `rewrite IH1` generates the premise subgoals; ordering can be tricky. Let me write explicitly:

```coq
  - f_equal.
    + apply IH1. 
      * intro Hin. apply Hfresh. apply in_or_app. left. exact Hin.
      * exact Hslc.
      * exact Hv.
    + apply IH2.
      * intro Hin. apply Hfresh. apply in_or_app. right. exact Hin.
      * exact Hslc.
      * exact Hv.
```

- abs case: goal after simpl: `tm_abs T (subst ... (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) t1)) = tm_abs T (open_rec (S k) v (subst sigma t1))`. `f_equal` then apply IH with k := S k ✓.
