Now env_ok:

```coq
Definition subst_lc (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall y, locally_closed (sigma y).

Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  subst_lc sigma /\ (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> LR T (sigma x)).
```

Lemma env_ok_update_fvar and env_ok_update as discussed.

```coq
Lemma env_ok_update_fvar : forall Gamma sigma x T1,
  env_ok Gamma sigma ->
  env_ok (update Gamma x T1) (subst_update sigma x (tm_fvar x)).
Proof.
  intros Gamma sigma x T1 [Hslc Henv]. split.
  - intros y. destruct (Nat.eq_dec x y) as [-> | Hne].
    + rewrite subst_update_same. apply lc_fvar.
    + rewrite subst_update_diff; [apply Hslc | exact Hne].
  - intros y T' Hy. destruct (Nat.eq_dec x y) as [-> | Hne].
    + rewrite subst_update_same. unfold update in Hy. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl in Hy.
      injection Hy as Hy. subst T'. apply fvar_LR.
    + rewrite subst_update_diff; [| exact Hne].
      apply Henv. unfold update in Hy.
      assert (E : (x =? y) = false) by (apply Nat.eqb_neq; exact Hne).
      rewrite E in Hy. exact Hy.
Qed.
```

Note `destruct (Nat.eq_dec x y) as [-> | Hne]` — in the first branch, the equation x = y substitutes. Which variable gets substituted? With `->`, Coq replaces... The pattern `->` uses the equation to rewrite; typically it substitutes y by x if the equation is x = y (it will eliminate the variable that is "younger"?). Hmm. Since both x and y are local variables (x introduced by intros before y? In this lemma, x comes from the statement and y from `intros y`), Coq's `->` will substitute y := x (it substitutes the variable that isn't in the other hypothesis... ). Actually the intro pattern `->` on an equality hypothesis H : x = y replaces y by x everywhere? Or x by y? The rule: `->` rewrites the goal using H left-to-right, i.e., replaces x with y? Hmm, subst-like behavior: In practice, `destruct (Nat.eq_dec x y) as [-> | Hne]` — I believe it substitutes y with x or x with y depending on which is a local variable that can be eliminated. Both are local variables... Coq's `subst` will substitute the one that appears... it can be unpredictable.

To be safe, handle both cases by using the lemmas with explicit arguments after the substitution. E.g., if the goal becomes `locally_closed (subst_update sigma x (tm_fvar x) x)` after y := x, then `subst_update_same` applies. If instead x := y, the goal is `locally_closed (subst_update sigma y (tm_fvar y) y)` — `subst_update_same sigma y (tm_fvar y)` also applies ✓ since it's stated for arbitrary x. Good, either way works.

But careful in the second part: `apply fvar_LR` requires the goal `LR T' (tm_fvar x)` or `LR T' (tm_fvar y)` — both fine ✓.

Hmm, but one more subtlety: if `->` substitutes x := y, then in Hy : update Gamma x T1 y = Some T'... it becomes update Gamma y T1 y = Some T' and `unfold update in Hy; rewrite Nat.eqb_refl in Hy` gives Some T1 = Some T' ✓ — same. Fine.

And if y := x, then Hy : update Gamma x T1 x = Some T' ✓.

OK. But `rewrite Nat.eqb_refl in Hy`: after unfold update, Hy : (if x =? x then Some T1 else Gamma x) = Some T' — hmm, `if Nat.eqb x x then...`. `rewrite Nat.eqb_refl in Hy` should reduce the if → `Some T1 = Some T'`? `Nat.eqb_refl : forall x, (x =? x) = true`. Rewriting in the `if` condition gives `if true then Some T1 else Gamma x`, which simplifies to Some T1 ✓ (rewrite does simplification of if-true automatically? `rewrite` on the condition of an if will reduce it ✓ I believe: rewriting with (x =? x) = true turns `if (x =? x) then a else b` into `if true then a else b` which is convertible to a; then `simpl in Hy` might be needed. Let me add `simpl in Hy` after.

Also `injection Hy as Hy` — Hy : Some T1 = Some T'. `injection Hy as Hy` gives Hy : T1 = T' ✓. Then `subst T'` ✓.

Hmm, one concern: `T'` is a variable introduced by `intros y T' Hy`. `subst T'` should work ✓.

Now the general env_ok_update:

```coq
Lemma env_ok_update : forall Gamma sigma x T1 v,
  env_ok Gamma sigma ->
  LR T1 v ->
  env_ok (update Gamma x T1) (subst_update sigma x v).
Proof.
  intros Gamma sigma x T1 v [Hslc Henv] Hv. split.
  - intros y. destruct (Nat.eq_dec x y) as [-> | Hne].
    + rewrite subst_update_same. apply LR_lc with (T := T1). exact Hv.
    + rewrite subst_update_diff; [apply Hslc | exact Hne].
  - intros y T' Hy. destruct (Nat.eq_dec x y) as [-> | Hne].
    + rewrite subst_update_same. unfold update in Hy. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl in Hy. simpl in Hy.
      injection Hy as Hy. subst T'. exact Hv.
    + rewrite subst_update_diff; [| exact Hne].
      apply Henv. unfold update in Hy.
      assert (E : (x =? y) = false) by (apply Nat.eqb_neq; exact Hne).
      rewrite E in Hy. exact Hy.
Qed.
```

Hmm, in the first branch, `rewrite subst_update_same` requires the term to be `subst_update sigma ?x v ?x` — depends on which variable got substituted. If y := x: goal `locally_closed (subst_update sigma x v x)` ✓ = v ✓. If x := y: goal `locally_closed (subst_update sigma y v y)` ✓ same lemma ✓.

But wait: if x := y is substituted, then `Hv : LR T1 v` unaffected ✓, and the goal `LR T' (subst_update sigma y v y)` → rewrite → LR T' v → exact Hv ✓.

Now the fundamental theorem. Let me write it:

```coq
(* ---------------- Fundamental theorem ---------------- *)

Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR T (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H.
  induction H as [Gamma x T Hx
                 | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHbody
                 | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
                 | Gamma
                 | Gamma].
  - (* T_Var *)
    intros sigma [Hslc Henv]. simpl. apply Henv. exact Hx.
  - (* T_Abs *)
    intros sigma [Hslc Henv].
    destruct (fresh_atom (L ++ fv t1)) as [x Hx].
    assert (HxL : ~ In x L).
    { intro Hin. apply Hx. apply in_or_app. left. exact Hin. }
    assert (Hxfv : ~ In x (fv t1)).
    { intro Hin. apply Hx. apply in_or_app. right. exact Hin. }
    assert (Henv_fx : env_ok (update Gamma x T1) (subst_update sigma x (tm_fvar x))).
    { apply env_ok_update_fvar. split; assumption. }
    assert (Hbody_fx : LR T2 (open (subst sigma t1) (tm_fvar x))).
    { assert (H0 := IHbody x HxL (subst_update sigma x (tm_fvar x)) Henv_fx).
      assert (Heq : subst (subst_update sigma x (tm_fvar x)) (open t1 (tm_fvar x))
                     = open (subst sigma t1) (tm_fvar x)).
      { apply subst_open_gen.
        - exact Hxfv.
        - exact Hslc.
        - apply lc_fvar. }
      rewrite Heq in H0. exact H0. }
    assert (Hlcabs : locally_closed (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1))).
    { apply lc_abs. apply lc_open_inv with (u := tm_fvar x) (k := 0).
      - apply LR_lc with (T := T2). exact Hbody_fx.
      - apply lc_fvar. }
    simpl. split.
    + exact Hlcabs.
    + split.
      * apply SN_abs.
      * intros s Hs.
        apply (LR_abs_app s (LR_SN T1 s Hs) T1 (subst sigma t1) T2 Hlcabs).
        -- intros s' Hs'.
           assert (Henv_s : env_ok (update Gamma x T1) (subst_update sigma x s')).
           { apply env_ok_update; [split; assumption | exact Hs']. }
           assert (H0 := IHbody x HxL (subst_update sigma x s') Henv_s).
           assert (Heq : subst (subst_update sigma x s') (open t1 (tm_fvar x))
                        = open (subst sigma t1) s').
           { apply subst_open_gen.
             - exact Hxfv.
             - exact Hslc.
             - apply LR_lc with (T := T1). exact Hs'. }
           rewrite Heq in H0. exact H0.
        -- exact Hs.
  - (* T_App *)
    intros sigma [Hslc Henv]. simpl.
    apply LR_app with (A := T1).
    + apply IH1. split; assumption.
    + apply IH2. split; assumption.
  - (* T_True *)
    intros sigma [Hslc Henv]. simpl. split.
    + apply lc_true.
    + apply SN_true.
  - (* T_False *)
    intros sigma [Hslc Henv]. simpl. split.
    + apply lc_false.
    + apply SN_false.
Qed.
```

Hmm, careful details:

1. In T_Var: `simpl` changes the goal from `LR T (subst sigma (tm_fvar x))` to `LR T (sigma x)` ✓; then `apply Henv` where Henv : forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> LR T (sigma x) ✓. Hmm, but I destructed Henv from the pair, so Henv is the second component ✓.

2. In T_Abs: `IHbody` has the type `forall x, ~ In x L -> forall sigma, env_ok (update Gamma x T1) sigma -> LR T2 (subst sigma (open t1 (fvar x)))` — as the induction principle shows: `(forall x, ~ In x L -> P (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2)`. Here P Gamma t T := forall sigma, env_ok Gamma sigma -> LR T (subst sigma t). So IHbody x HxL : forall sigma, env_ok (update Gamma x T1) sigma -> LR T2 (subst sigma (open t1 (fvar x))) ✓.

3. `assert (H0 := IHbody x HxL (subst_update sigma x (tm_fvar x)) Henv_fx)` ✓.

4. In the subst_open_gen application, `open t1 (tm_fvar x)` should be definitionally `open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t1` ✓.

5. `simpl` in T_Abs: goal `LR (T1 → T2) (subst sigma (tm_abs T1 t1))` → `locally_closed (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) /\ strongly_normalizing (...) /\ (forall s, ...)`. ✓

6. `apply (LR_abs_app s (LR_SN T1 s Hs) T1 (subst sigma t1) T2 Hlcabs)` — the lemma LR_abs_app : forall s, SN s -> forall A body B, lc -> (forall s', LR A s' -> LR B (open body s')) -> LR A s -> LR B (app (abs A body) s). With s := s, the SN proof, A := T1, body := subst sigma t1, B := T2, Hlcabs, then two remaining goals: the opening hypothesis and LR T1 s ✓. The conclusion `LR T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) s)` matches the goal ✓ (the goal after simpl is `LR T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) s)`).

Careful: `apply (LR_abs_app s (LR_SN T1 s Hs) T1 (subst sigma t1) T2 Hlcabs)` — passing arguments positionally: the second explicit argument after s is the SN proof ✓, then A, body, B, Hlcabs ✓, then the two remaining premises become goals ✓.

7. In the T_App case, `apply LR_app with (A := T1)` — LR_app : forall A B t s, LR (Arrow A B) t -> LR A s -> LR B (app t s). The goal after simpl: `LR T2 (tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2))` ✓ B := T2 automatically ✓ leaves LR (T1→T2) (subst sigma t1) and LR T1 (subst sigma t2) ✓.

Note: `simpl` in T_App might not reduce `subst sigma (tm_app t1 t2)` to the app form? It should ✓.

8. The destruct pattern `intros sigma [Hslc Henv]` — env_ok is a conjunction of subst_lc and the pointwise property ✓.

Now the final theorem:

```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T H.
  assert (Henv : env_ok empty (fun x => tm_fvar x)).
  { split.
    - intros y. apply lc_fvar.
    - intros x T' Hx. simpl in Hx. discriminate. }
  assert (HLR := fundamental empty t T H (fun x => tm_fvar x) Henv).
  rewrite subst_id in HLR.
  apply LR_SN with (T := T). exact HLR.
Qed.

```
Inductive strongly_normalizing : tm -> Prop :=
